u, then c s g wh ch) with one word that doesn't
carry it; spelling-choice circles (post offi[s/ss/
c]e, oran[g/dg/j]e, bla[k/ck/c], cit[ey/ss/y]…);
match sentence halves about identity (**family
name** and **dad's name** enter here: "My family
name is ___ · My dad's name is ___"), then complete
with your own information, then interview a partner
and write His/Her answers; match capitals to smalls
in a jumble; write the capitals (the list runs a–y
— q, x, z absent, as printed); L7 a letter dictation
page ("Write the letters your teacher tells you.")
+ an alphabetically ordered copy list.
**Revision 1** — first-letter completion against an
alphabet strip; "Think and complete" — find the
shared beginning or ending across word sets (-er:
broth__, jump__, riv__, rubb__, rul__, sist__,
wat__…); and a full new dialog to complete — Ali
introduces his brother: "Ali: Hello, Jack. Jack:
Hello, Ali. Ali: Jack. This is ___ brother, Mousa.
Jack: Hello, Mousa. Mousa: Hello. ___ you English,
Jack? Jack: Yes, I am. Mousa: ___ are you from in
England? Jack: London. Mousa: London! ___'s my
favourite city. Jack: Really? My favourite city is
Sirt! Mousa: Where's ___ house in Sirt? Jack: It
___ near your house. Mousa: Is it? Jack: Yes. It's
___ Beach Road. **Drink, Mousa? Mousa: Yes, please.
Lemonade, please. Jack: What about ___, Ali? Ali:
Lemonade too, please.**"
**U5** — build the possession words from syllable
pieces across three columns (ca+me+ra = camera the
worked example); name each category row and add 2
more words of your own; **picture equations** —
[boy] + [watch] + ✓ = "Ali has got a watch.", with
✗ and ? building the negative and the question;
read and mark a ✓/✗ grid; ownership sentences
("The TV is Ahmed's."); a Q&A match that fixes
Ali's family in this book (1 brother **Mousa**, 1
sister **Huda**, matching the Pupils U7 text); a
possessions crossword (shaded column: has got…);
**spelling-by-ear dialogs** — "What's this in
English? — It's a pen. — With a B? — No, with a P."
· bee "B-E? — No, B-E-E." · "No, not pensil. It's
P-E-N-C-I-L. — With a C? — That's right." · egg
"E-G? — No, E-G-G." · "His name's Ted. — D-E-D? —
No, T-E-D."; odd one out with **"Find the reason"**
(each row matched to "It isn't a colour." / "It
isn't a drink." / family word / fruit / room /
adjective / animal / clothing); the Pupils ages
recalled and ordered; write the full form / write
the short form over Nisrin's whole self-introduction
("I am 10. I am Libyan. I am from Tripoli. I have
got a brother and a sister. My brother's name is
Tarek. He is 11. He is tall. My sister's name is
Amna. She is 8. She is short."); trace x; L7 a
**numbers lesson** — sums to solve and say (3 + 3,
2 + 7, 4 + 4, 6 + 5, 11 + 1, 9 + 1), read and copy
one–twelve in words, "What's missing?" (four,
eight, eleven), "What's next?" sequences (counting,
evens, odds, backwards, doubling).
**U6** — opposites match (get up/go to bed,
start/finish); rebus clock sentences (picture verbs
+ clock faces); the Pupils clock diagram as a
label-it-yourself page; draw the hands; make
questions from statements; complete the negatives;
more spelling dialogs (Jack J-A-C-K not G-A-K,
jacket J-A-C-K-E-T not J-A-K-E-T, orange with a G
not a J, eight E-I-G-H-T not A-I-G-H-T, have
H-A-V-E not H-A-V); the category-question frame
sorting a/an; the school-times dialog completed
from a bank; order the day's actions; full/short
forms over the bicycle text ("I do not get up
early. My school is not near my house, but I have
got a bicycle. It is a big bicycle. It is green.");
write qu; L7 order the times, write 1–12 in words,
copy A–E, **alphabetical order** (apple, big, cat,
desk, egg).
**U7** — activities wordsearch (table tennis found
diagonally); **play/go matching** (play football,
basketball, table tennis · go cycling, fishing,
shopping, swimming); answer about yourself with
short answers (Yes, I do. / No, I don't. / Yes, I
have. / No, I haven't.); "Can you remember …" —
category recall with rising counts (one adjective,
two animals, three clothes, four drinks, five
colours, six rooms, seven fruits, eight places);
draw and guess; sentence clouds with place phrases;
**written riddles** ("What is it? 1. It's big and
tall. It's yellow and black. It's an animal. 2.
It's yellow, gold or white. It's near the sea. It's
hot. It's a place. 3. It's got a kitchen, a
bathroom, a toilet and four bedrooms. 4. It's brown
or grey. It's small. It's in the classroom. 5. It's
got milk and bananas and bubbles. It's a drink.");
a likes listening grid (☺ = likes, ☹ = doesn't
like) checked against a full paragraph about Ahmed
("…He hasn't got a bicycle **because** he doesn't
like cycling."); join the halves (tele+vision,
o'+clock, just+after…); a **pair spelling game** —
Pupil A spells from a capital list (BAG, BED, CAT,
IN, DATE, WHAT, THAT, BIKE, JACKET, CITY), Pupil B
writes, then swapped (BATH, BIG, GET, AT, JUICE,
WHITE, WATCH, HE, BEACH, UP); the shop dialog as
choose-the-correct-response (one line here says
"green, either" where both Pupils printings say
blue — as printed) + comprehension questions + a
**Study box that names the pattern**: "like + like,
too · not like + not like, either"; complete
too/either/but sentences from a Nisrin–Ali grid;
capitals matching (all 26); "Write the correct
form" (i = I, ali = Ali, libya = Libya, sirt =
Sirt); **correct the following** — a new boy's text
to fix ("my name is yasir i am libyan i am from
tripoli i have got two brothers their names are
shareef and nabil"); "Write about yourself."; L7 a
number-words crossword whose shaded column spells a
hidden number + letter-name groups + alphabetical
order.
**U8** — match 13–20; **-teen as sums in words**
("three + ten = thirteen", then four + ten …
nine + ten) and **-teen as spelling sums** ("4 + 10
+ e = fourteen (four te en)" · "5 ~~ve~~ + f + 10 +
e = fifteen (fi~~ve~~ f te en)" · "8 ~~t~~ + 10 + e
= eighteen" · "3 ~~ree~~ + ir + 10 + e = thirteen"
— the changed letters crossed out on the page);
unscramble the game rules; match sentence halves; a
**read-and-solve game story** ("I'm on one and I
get a three. I go up the ladder. I spin again and
I get a three. I go up the ladder. I spin again and

draw the time; the spinner steps completed from a
verb bank; the pair spelling game again; **read and
draw** — nine instruction steps that build a picture
("1. Draw two small squares. 2. Draw a big square on

find-the-differences); order the times; L7 column

**Revision 2** — "Look at Pupil's Book page 56.
What's missing?" (the sports-centre picture in

# Libya — English Grade 7 (Preparatory 1): Course Book, WorkBook

Record check first, per the G4 lesson: the grade-07
record names a **Course Book + WorkBook** pair, not
Pupils/Activity — `07_English_for_Libya_Course_
Book.pdf` and `07_English_for_Libya_WorkBook.pdf`
at liasinstitute.com. Both downloaded as real PDFs
(Course Book 11,406,428 B, 88 pp; WorkBook
4,499,265 B, 104 pp). The 146-byte scare belonged
to a guessed Pupils_Book URL, not to the record.

### Grade 7 Course Book

88 pp, image-only — **READ COMPLETE 2026-10-25**
(read in full from rendered pages; **book p. = PDF
p. − 2**). Identity: "ENGLISH FOR LIBYA —
**Preparatory 1** — Course Book — Jenny Quintana,
Helen O'Neill and Fiona McGarry", State of Libya,
Ministry of Education, CERC; imprint "Materials
development by **Garnet Publishing Ltd**… **First
published 2007, Reprinted 2018**", code **LP1 CB
18**; school year 2019–2020 (1440–1441 AH). So the
Preparatory line is the same 2007 Garnet course as
Primary 6 (Quintana again), reprinted — while
Primary 1–5 carry the new 2019 "21st Century"
course. Shape: **ten units of eight lessons each**
(lessons numbered 1.1–10.8, exercises lettered A, B,
C…, Look! boxes as before), a two-page scope table
up front (topic / vocabulary areas / language /
functions / example skills activities per unit —
kept in this map's unit lines), real photographs
mixed with drawings, and two ruled writing pages at
the end. Cast: **Hasan (12, Tripoli)** and his
sister **Nisrin (5)**; **Ali** — 11 in 1.1, **twelve
in his own 1.8 story** (both as printed) — with
brothers **Khalid/Khaled (9)** and **Mustafa (14)**;
their father **Mohammed**, a doctor, and mother
**Laila** (named in 9.8); **Aisha, Fatima, Hala,
Alia, Sameera, Salwa, Huda, Amna**; **Omar, Ahmed,
Naser, Fahmi, Abdullah, Sam**; and the English
children **Jack** (cousins **Lucy 13, James 15,
Adam 19**; grandparents in Manchester, uncle and
aunt in London) and **Joe, Tom**.

Contents: U1 Hobbies and routines (p.5) · U2 Family
and jobs (p.13) · U3 Likes and dislikes (p.21) · U4
At home and around town (p.29) · U5 Clothes and
shopping (p.37) · U6 Animals and nature (p.45) · U7
Past and present (p.53) · U8 Food and health (p.61)
· U9 Dates and birthdays (p.69) · U10 A long time
ago (p.77, the revision unit).

**U1** — 1.1 I'm Hasan (self, sister, friends; the
third question is new: **Where do you live?**). 1.2
Times of day: morning/afternoon/evening/night by
sun pictures; Hasan's family in six third-person
sentences ("Hasan walks to school at seven o'clock
in the morning." · "Nisrin does not go to school.
She plays at home in the morning." · "Her brother
does his homework."); Look!: **in** the morning /
afternoon / evening but **at** night. 1.3 activity
phrases (go swimming, cycle, listen to music,
watch TV, play table tennis, go shopping, read, do
homework) + Jack's and Lucy's routines ("I cycle
to school every morning. I play table tennis or go
swimming in the afternoon. I do my homework late
in the evening." · "I read books in the afternoon
and sometimes I go shopping…"). 1.4 Aisha's day in
picture-clock sentences with **figure times**
(starts school 7.30, home 1.15, music 3.15,
shopping 4.45, homework 8.30 — three captioned, the
rest to make). 1.5 days of the week **Saturday-
first** (Saturday…Friday); "What day is it today?
— It's Wednesday."; a Saturday-first Timetable
(five sports + Friday "go to the mosque"); first
**they** ("They play football every Wednesday.").
1.6 an interview printed in two split columns
(questions one side, answers the other): "What
games do you play? — I play basketball. — When do
you play? — On Tuesdays and Thursdays. — Where do
you play? — At school." + a second about reading
and cycling; Look!: "every day · every Tuesday =
on Tuesdays". 1.7 **can/can't** for ability: ten
phrases (swim, ride a bike, make cakes, drive a
car, fly, paint pictures, play tennis, sing a
song, speak English, read and write English);
Look!: "He can drive. He can't ride a bike. She
can paint. It can swim."; "Can you …? — Yes, I can.
/ No, I can't." 1.8 **Ali's autobiography** in
full: "My name is Ali. I am twelve years old and I
live in Tripoli. I have two brothers. Their names
are Khalid and Mustafa. Khalid is nine and Mustafa
is fourteen. We live in a big house near a park. I
like going to the park on Fridays with my family.
I get up at quarter past six every day. After
breakfast, I go to school with my friend, Hasan. I
play table tennis on Mondays and football on
Wednesdays. Football is my favourite game. I like
riding my bike, too. I can ride a bike, but I
can't ride a horse." + build-your-own questions
from stubs (How old …? How many …? When does …?).

**U2** — 2.1 have got over Aisha's family ("Have
you got any sisters? — Yes, I've got an older
sister. She's fourteen… I've got one younger
brother. He's ten." · "Who else is in your family?
— Just my mother and father. I haven't got any
grandparents." · two cars — "My father's got a big
car and my mother's got a small one." · "We've got
a big flat."); Look! full→short as an **arrow**:
"I have got → I've got · She has got → She's got ·
My father has got → My father's got · I have not
got → I haven't got". 2.2 Jack's family (grand-
parents, grandfather, grandmother, uncle, aunt,
cousins — Lucy 13, James 15, Adam 19) + questions.
2.3 jobs (doctor, teacher, policeman, farmer,
student, fireman, shopkeeper, nurse) matched to
faces; "What does your father do? — He's a
farmer." 2.4 jobs again + their tools pictured
(cap, stethoscope, thermometer, scales, book bag,
plans and pen, tractor, tape measure): "Whose is
this? — It's the teacher's." 2.5 **It's mine!** —
a bag-and-ruler dialog; Look! arrow: "It's my pen
→ It's mine · your → yours · his → his · her →
hers". 2.6 A doctor's day: order six pictures of
Mohammed's day, retell with given words; Look!
frequency placement: "He **always** gets up early.
He **sometimes** reads the newspaper. He **never**
drinks coffee." 2.7 Ali is ill: headache,
stomachache, temperature + the mother dialog in
full ("What's the matter, Ali? — I feel ill. —
Have you got a headache? — No, I haven't. — Have
you got a stomachache? — No, I haven't. — Are you
hot? Have you got a temperature? — Yes, I think
so."). 2.8 Who am I?: four first-person job
riddles + a guess-who game about the class.

**U3** — 3.1 numbers: tens to **a hundred** on a
speedometer; sums spoken in words ("Fifteen plus
seven **equals** twenty-two."), eight printed sums
to judge right or wrong (two are wrong on purpose:
20 + 13 = 35, 30 + 26 = 29). 3.2 How much is it?:
numbers to hundreds and **halves** (173, 258, 369,
428, 7½, 9½); Look!: "101 = a hundred and one ·
200 = two hundred · 5½ = five and a half" and "LD
= Libyan Dinars"; priced goods (bicycle 80 LD,
television 120 LD, radio 21 LD, computer 400 LD,
CD player 30 LD, trousers 12 LD, camera 48 LD,
watch 15 LD, CDs 1 LD **each**, motorbike 400 LD);
"How much is the television? — It's 120 LD. · How
much are the CDs? — They're 1 LD each." 3.3
**British money**: real coin pictures (1 penny ·
2, 5, 10, 20, 50 pence · 1 pound = 100 pence) +
Lucy's shop dialog, which ends in a sum ("Can I
have a chocolate bar and two apples, please? —
Here you are. That's …" — 40p + 2 × 30p; "How much
does Lucy pay?"). 3.4 **Can I borrow …?** — three
dialogs to complete with three refusal lines ("No,
sorry. It's my brother's." · "I need it." · "No,
sorry. I only have one."). 3.5 like + -ing about
seven pictured people, matched by clues ("She
likes going to the beach, but she doesn't like
swimming."). 3.6 **collocations**: play / ride /
go / make matched to shopping, cakes, a bike,
computer games, a horse, volleyball, salads, to
the beach; "Do you like playing table tennis? —
Yes, I do. / No, I don't."; report your partner to
the class. 3.7 **want to / Let's**: Hasan–Ali
dialog + a conversation frame (Ask: Do you want
to …? · Answer: No, it's too cold / too hot / I
don't like … / I can't … · Suggest: Let's …). 3.8
three trip adverts as reading (Beach trip · City
trip · Lake trip) matched to picture strips.

**U4** — 4.1 Ali's room: find the furniture; new
positions **behind, in front of, between, under**;
ten read-and-point sentences; Look!: "What **is**
it? / What **are** they?" 4.2 Fatima's house
cutaway + her text in full: "I live in a house in
a village. Our house is big and it is near the
sea. **Downstairs**, there is a kitchen, a dining
room and a sitting room. **Upstairs**, there are
three bedrooms, a bathroom and a toilet. You can
see my father, my mother and my three brothers in
this picture." (the matching answers also use
"living room" for the same room — both words in
the book). 4.3 a block town plan (bookshop,
toyshop, cinema, car park, bank, café,
supermarket, block of flats): six sentences, **correct the three wrong ones**; then a memory game
with the partner's book closed. 4.4 travel: by car
/ by bus / walks / cycles / by taxi / runs matched
to people (Alia, Khaled, Sameera, Hasan, Laila,
Omar); "How do you travel to school? / the
mosque? / the mall?" 4.5 Signs: Turn left · Turn
right · Go straight on; Look! imperatives: "Turn
right. / **Don't** turn right. · Run. / **Don't**
run or **No** running."; prohibition signs talked
about ("Don't park here."). 4.6 eight town
buildings matched (mosque, book shop, cinema,
bank, park, supermarket, post office, café); talk
frames ("There's a supermarket, but there isn't a
cinema." · "The supermarket is in the town
centre."). 4.7 a town map + the directions
exchange in full: "Excuse me. Can you tell me the
way to the post office? — Oh, yes. It's not far.
Walk along this street. Turn left at the
supermarket. Go past the café and then cross the
road. Turn right and go straight on. The post
office is on the left." (the intro names Fatima
visiting Salwa; the bubbles give the asking to
Amna — as printed). 4.8 **The teacher's letter**:
a dialog story — the teacher asks Ali to take a
letter to the bank ("Can you take this letter to
the bank, please? — Yes, of course, sir. — Thank
you. Don't forget. It's important."), Abdullah's
new bike distracts him, the letter is lost —
"Where's my teacher's letter? I know! It's …" —
and the class **chooses the best ending** (At
school. / At Abdullah's. / At the bank.).

**U5** — 5.1 clothes grow (trousers, jacket, belt,
shirt, scarf, tie, skirt, jumper, T-shirt +
trainers, pairs); "What's it like? — It's a red
tie. · What are they like? — I've got an old pair
and a new pair."; Look! arrow: "The tie is red. →
It's a **red tie**." 5.2 **wearing**: "Ali's shirt
is white. → He's wearing a white shirt. His
trousers are blue. → He's wearing blue trousers.";
identify Ali/Hasan/Ahmed and Fatima/Hala/Alia from
descriptions; a second Look! shows **lists joined
with commas and and** — the repeated "He is
wearing" crossed out line by line: "He is wearing
a blue shirt, white socks and black shoes." 5.3
What's happening …?: eight "now" pictures + the
book's first full grammar table, **Present
continuous tense** (I am/am not · You/We/They
are/are not · He/She/It is/is not + -ing; questions
Am/Are/Is … drinking water?). 5.4 a café scene
read-and-find text ("It's 7 o'clock in the
evening. The boys are in a café. Ali is wearing
blue trousers and a white shirt. He is drinking
orange juice. Ahmed is wearing a purple shirt and
grey trousers. He is eating an apple.") + close-
your-book memory questions. 5.5 Choosing clothes:
three want/get dialogs (too big / too expensive /
too small — "What does Fatima **want**? What does
she **get**?"); Look! **this / these / that /
those** drawn as pointing hands, near and far. 5.6
**was**: five clock-and-place sentences ("I was in
the café at quarter to six." · "I was at school at
two o'clock." · "I was at home at four o'clock." ·
"I was in the shoe shop at quarter past five." ·
"I was in the supermarket at half past five.");
Look!: **at** home, **at** school, **in** the shoe
shop. 5.7–5.8 **The yellow shirt**: a two-page
comic story — Omar and Ahmed shop for a shirt
(twenty dinars too expensive; a yellow one at six
dinars bought), and at home the shirt is too big:
the shop mixed them up ("Oh no! Look, Ahmed! It's
too big. I've got that man's shirt." · "And that
man's got your shirt!") — then "Act out the
story."

**U6** — 6.1 body parts (head, neck, arm, hand,
leg, feet) on a person + **real photographs** (fish,
tiger, monkey, elephant, snake, giraffe) +
description riddles ("It's tall. It's got a long
neck and a small head. It's got four long legs.
It can run fast, but it can't fly. What is it?" ·
"It's big and dangerous. It's got black stripes. ·
It's small and orange. It's got two eyes, but it
hasn't got any ears or feet. It can swim but it
can't fly."). 6.2 more photo animals (butterfly,
rabbit, goat, horse, cheetah, elephant) + the
guessing dialog (Has it got four legs? — Yes, it
has. · Can it run? — Yes, it can. · Is it a rabbit?
— No, it isn't! · Is it an …? — Yes, it is!);
Look! the three It frames (It is / It can / It has
got, statement and question). 6.3 Where do they
live?: habitat sentences matched to photos
(baboons, mountain goats, camels — "They can live
in very hot, dry places." · "They eat plants,
grass, paper and even clothes!" · "They usually
live in trees and eat fruit and nuts."); Look! the
full frequency ladder (always / usually / often /
sometimes / never). 6.4 In the desert: a
description text with questions about its picture
+ the unit's key Look!: "What **do** camels **do**?
Camels **drink** water. (always) · What **are** the
camels **doing**? Two camels **are drinking**
water. (in the picture)" — always-true against
in-the-picture-now. 6.5 weather: sun, cloud, rain,
snow, wind, wet, dry; "What's the weather like?" —
"The sun is shining. · A wind is blowing. · It is
snowing. · There are clouds in the sky. · It is
raining."; Look! **noun → adjective** table (wind
→ windy, sun → sunny, rain → rainy, cloud →
cloudy). 6.6 seasons: four England pictures;
"There are four seasons in England." then Talk
about Libya, season by season, + favourite season.
6.7 months on **two season wheels — England and
Australia**, the Australian seasons reversed;
"There are twelve months in a year. January is
the first month and December is the last." 6.8 a
six-question **Nature quiz** (multiple choice with
photos: a butterfly has six legs; cheetahs run
very fast; goats sometimes eat paper; snakes like
hot, dry places; plants start growing in spring;
leaves change colour in autumn).

**U7** — 7.1 **past simple opens**: time phrases
(This morning · Yesterday afternoon · Last
Saturday · Last Monday · On Tuesday evening ·
Last Friday) matched to -ed sentences (played,
watched, listened, visited, phoned, prayed) over a
Friday-to-Thursday calendar; "What did Hasan do
yesterday afternoon? — He played football." 7.2
**Omar's diary** in full (Wednesday: school,
football with Ahmed and Ali, home, TV; Thursday:
the doctor's, home, telephoned friends, computer
games with his brother) + Was/Did questions with
short answers (Yes, he was. / No, he wasn't. / Yes,
he did.); question-building from fragments; Look!
present → past arrow (am/is → was, are → were, do
→ did). 7.3 Present and past: six paired panels,
the verb in red in both tenses — **drink/drank,
do/did, have/had, buy/bought, go/went,
drives/drove**; make both questions ("What does
Ali drink in the morning?" / "What did he drink
this morning?"). 7.4 the beach conversation in
full (Omar interviews Ahmed: boat, Hasan's father
**caught** some fish — catch/caught captioned by
the picture — cooked the fish, bread and salad,
drank lots of water, a long walk, home at five,
homework; sequencers First… Then… after that) +
a guessing game (Did you watch TV? — No, I
didn't.); Look!: didn't = did not. 7.5 opinions:
"What did you think of **it**? — I liked **it**.
**It** was very interesting." / "I didn't like it.
It was too sweet." / "…too long." — **it** printed
in blue with "Look at the blue words. What do
they mean?"; Look!: very sweet against too sweet.
7.6 The park: Mustafa's story in five clock
panels (left home at quarter to five, walked to
the park, waited — "He waited for an hour. His
friends didn't come." — home at six, phoned Ahmed
at seven: "Where were you?") + guess Ahmed's
excuse from four lines, listen and check +
questions (How long did he wait?). 7.7 The
castle: a **real photograph of Tripoli's castle**
(the Red Castle on the harbour) + Fatima's day
out as a **picture-rebus text** — the verbs are
pictures to be spoken in the past (car = drove,
museum, walking figures = walked, smiley = liked,
banknote = bought, eyes = watched, book = read,
cooking pot = cooked): "Last Thursday, Fatima's
father [drove] to Tripoli with Fatima and her
brothers. First, they went to the [museum] and
looked at the old pots and jewellery. Then they
[walked] around the castle. Fatima [liked] the
jewellery best. After that, they went to the park
and [bought] some ice-creams. In the evening, they
went home and Fatima [watched] a film on TV while
her father [read] the newspaper and her mother
[cooked] dinner." + close the book and retell.
7.8 A new job: reorder the six doctor's-day
pictures (the 2.6 art again) and retell; Look!
sequencers (First, he… Next, he… Then, he…
Finally, he…) + the spelling trap (present read →
past **read**, same spelling).

**U8** — 8.1 **countable / uncountable** as a
two-column picture table headed "Things you can
count / Things you can't count" (sweets, an egg,
four tomatoes, two bananas, a melon — chicken,
rice, salad, cheese); Look!: an egg · three eggs ·
some eggs · some salad · some cheese; "Can I have
an apple, please? — Yes, you can. / No, sorry.
They're finished." 8.2 In the café: menu words +
a four-panel ordering story; Look! containers (a
cup of tea, a glass of juice, a bottle of water, a
packet of biscuits); the waiter's repeat-back
("That's one apple juice, …") to complete. 8.3
**some / any**: Jack and Sam's cycling picnic
dialog in full ("There's some cheese and a bottle
of water. — Are there any sandwiches? — No, there
aren't any sandwiches, but there is a packet of
biscuits. — Oh, good. Is there any juice? — No,
there isn't, but there's some fruit. There's a
**bunch of** grapes and two apples."); Look! the
three-line rule (There are **some** sweets. ·
There aren't **any** sweets. · Are there **any**
sweets?). 8.4 Making a cake: ingredients; **much /
lots of** inside the mother dialog ("There are
lots of eggs and I've got a bag of flour. Oh, dear!
There isn't any milk… Yes, there's not much oil.
Can you get a litre, please? And I need a kilo of
apples."); Look! measures (a bag of flour, a kilo
of apples, a litre of oil, a carton of milk); pick
Ali's true shopping list from three. 8.5 On
holiday in Italy: Jack's phone call home in full
(present continuous for now — "Are you having a
good time?" — Italy hot and sunny against England
"horrible — raining and cold", "Don't eat too
much ice-cream. Eat lots of healthy fruit and
salad."); five adjectives to guess from the talk
(lucky, delicious, healthy, fantastic, horrible);
Look! in pictures: lots of fruit · **too many**
cakes · **too much** homework. 8.6 a **Health
questionnaire**: six multiple-choice questions
(How often do you eat salad and vegetables? —
every day / two or three times a week / never …),
then ask your partner, then the teacher says how
healthy you are. 8.7 chores: eight phrases (make
lunch, tidy the bedroom, sweep the floor, clean
the kitchen, water the plants, wash the dishes,
answer the telephone, look after the baby) +
frequency with **hardly ever** added (always /
often / sometimes / hardly ever / never). 8.8
**I'll sweep the floor**: **will** for offers made
on the spot, in a family dialog in full (Father is
taking Mum to the hospital; Salwa: "I'll make
lunch and clean the kitchen." · Aisha: "I'll sweep
the floor." · "I'll look after you, Naser. We'll
water the plants." · "Can someone answer the
phone? — I'll do it.").

**U9** — 9.1 dates on a May calendar (Saturday-
first columns): ordinals **first to twentieth**
listed, compounds spoken (the twenty-fourth of
May, the thirty-first of May); "It's my birthday
on 6th May." · "We're going to the lake for the
weekend on the twenty fifth of May."; "What day is
the fifth of May? — It's a Friday." · "What is the
date today?" 9.2 **Hala's invitation letter** in
full, with the reading move taught first — look at
the questions, then read: "Dear Fatima, Would you
like to come to my party? My birthday is on
Saturday the sixth of May, but the party is on
Sunday the seventh. It starts at four o'clock and
it finishes at six o'clock. My house is next to
the post office. You can bring a friend. Best
wishes, Hala". 9.3 suggestions: a full Fatima–
Aisha dialog + a **named function table**,
"Suggesting things to do" (Would you like to /
Shall we / Let's × go / play × shopping? / to the
park? / a game.). 9.4–9.5 Choosing a present: a
shop window to search (a doll, toys, a box of
paints, a scarf, a belt, a book, a chess game, a
notebook, sandals) + the choosing dialog ("What
shall I buy for Hala? — How about a toy? — Hala
doesn't play with toys. She's thirteen. — OK. How
about a belt? — No. It's too expensive. — That
scarf looks nice. — No. I don't like it. — All
right. How about a box of paints? — That's a good
idea! She likes painting. I'll buy the paints.")
+ refusal frames (It's too … · He doesn't like … ·
She doesn't play with …). 9.6 At the weekend: Sam
and Joe hear the forecast ("Good news! It **will
be** sunny and hot all over England this
weekend.") and plan with will (we'll ask Tom…
we'll walk… "How long will it take? — About two
hours." "I'll take my fishing rod." "I'll go and
phone Tom."); a lake picture carrying real signs
(PLEASE HAVE YOUR PICNIC HERE · FISHING ·
SWIMMING · TOILETS). 9.7 At the lake: the planned
trip as an **eight-picture wordless story** (Sam
falls, hurts his foot, can't walk or be carried;
Tom goes for help; a truck is stopped; they drive
home) told from a phrase bank — **couldn't**
arrives inside it. 9.8 **Mohammed's letter** from
England in full (dated "Wednesday 31st May": "Dear
Laila — My plane arrived yesterday at eleven
o'clock. I went into London by underground train.
That was interesting. It took about an hour. I am
staying at a very nice hotel in West London. I met
Dr Williams for lunch today and he showed me the
hospital. Tomorrow I am going to the conference.
I'll phone on Friday to tell you about it. I want
to visit my cousin on Sunday. He lives near here
and I can go there on a London bus! Say 'hello' to
the children. I'll see you all next Tuesday. —
Mohammed") with a real photograph of a London
underground platform + six When questions — past,
present and future inside one real letter.

**U10 A long time ago (revision)** — 10.1 three
real-style posters to read for facts (DINOSAUR
EXHIBITION, 15th March – 30th June at the Science
Museum, open 9.30–5.30 Monday–Saturday · Kite
festival at Windy Hill Park, FREE ENTRY, 4th May,
11.45 onwards · Bird SAFARI at the zoo, OPEN
10.00–7.00, under fives go free) — the questions
live in the WorkBook. 10.2 Jack and Lucy negotiate
a day out against the weather, re-using the year's
suggestion language ("I'd like to go to the kite
festival."). 10.3 dinosaurs: anagrams first (ital
→ tail, cenk → neck, veeals → leaves, lntaps →
plants), then two fact texts with measurements
("Dinosaurs lived a long time ago. This dinosaur
was very big. It was about **twenty metres** long.
It had four legs. It had a long tail, a long neck
and a small head. It ate leaves and plants." ·
"This dinosaur was about **thirteen metres** long.
It had two legs and two small arms. It had a big
head. It ate meat."). 10.4 a museum floor plan
(plant room, dinosaur room, animal room, Roman
room, Egyptian room, cafe, shop, entrance) with
photos (an old pot, a fossil, a cup of coffee) +
directions talk ("Excuse me, can you tell me the
way to the dinosaur exhibition? — Yes, it's next
to the plant room. Go straight on past the
Egyptian room and the Roman room."). 10.5 life
events as present/past pairs (is born / was born ·
move / moved house · fall / fell off) + Ali's life
in dialog ("When I was two, my brother Khaled was
born. I started school when I was six… When I was
seven, we moved house… I got a bike when I was
eight. When I was nine, I fell off it and hurt my
arm."); Look!: "You can join two ideas with
**When**." 10.6 Photo album: a beach photograph
talked about in the present continuous. 10.7–10.8
six **real photographs** of places (at home · at
school · at the doctor's · at the café · at the
clothes shop · at the cinema) + six mini-
conversations — one per earlier unit (borrowing,
a film opinion, a price, a shall-offer of help, a
café order, the doctor) — to match to the
pictures: the year's functions in one round-up.

**Libya Grade 7 Course Book — READ COMPLETE
2026-10-25.**

### Grade 7 WorkBook

104 pp, image-only (text extraction 290 chars) —
**READ COMPLETE 2026-10-25** (read in full from
rendered pages, all 104; **book p. = PDF p.** —
the folios match the PDF pages directly, unlike
the Course Book). Identity: "ENGLISH FOR LIBYA —
**Preparatory 1 — Workbook** — Jenny Quintana,
Helen O'Neill and Fiona McGarry", State of Libya,
Ministry of Education, CERC; imprint "English for
Libya, Preparatory 1, Workbook — Materials
development by Garnet Publishing Ltd, 8 Southern
Court, South Street, Reading RG1 4QS, UK — First
published 2007, Reprinted 2018", code **LP1 WB
18**; school year 2019–2020 (1440–1441 AH). The
writing twin of the Course Book: every exercise
is keyed to a Course Book lesson (1.1–10.8) and
most lessons get exactly one workbook page.
Contents: U1 Hobbies and routines (p.5) · U2
Family and jobs (p.12) · U3 Likes and dislikes
(p.20) · U4 At home and around town (p.27) · U5
Clothes and shopping (p.35) · U6 Animals and
nature (p.42) · U7 Past and present (p.50) · U8
Food and health (p.57) · U9 Dates and birthdays
(p.66) · U10 A long time ago (p.74) · Sound and
spelling practice + Student A/B material (pp.81–
82) · Tapescript (pp.83–89) · Wordlists —
Alphabetical (pp.90–96) and Thematic (to ~p.103);
last page blank. The workbook's recurring moves:
missing-vowel spelling, anagrams, word-order
building, "correct the false sentences", true/
false against the Course Book by page reference,
and every unit ending in writing about yourself.

**Unit 1 (pp.5–11).** 1.1: pronoun cloze built
from the Course Book's own opening sentences
(his/My/Her/our/I/She/we/Their) + reorder the
three greetings questions. 1.2: match figures to
number words 1–12; write the times from four
clock pictures (a digital watch among them).
1.3: missing vowels (g_ sh_pp_ng, cycl_, pl_y
t_bl_ t_nn_s, d_ h_m_w_rk, w_tch TV, r__d) +
write your morning/afternoon/evening. 1.4: the
pair information-gap (Student A p.81 / Student B
p.82 — answers in the back matter below) +
sentences about your own routine. 1.5: write the
days from circled letters in Saturday-first rows;
sort six activities under **go** or **play**; ask
a partner from a pictured week timetable (Sat
play basketball · Mon play table tennis · Tue
play volleyball · Wed play football · Fri go to
the mosque) and write the sentences ("He goes to
the mosque every Friday."). 1.6: **Present tense
verb tables** — be/go/play/like across I, You,
He/She/It, We, They — then rewrite Lucy's spoken
"I play basketball on Mondays. I like basketball.
I go swimming on Thursdays. I don't like
swimming." in the third person. 1.7: number the
ability pictures (the phrase set here includes
**speak Arabic**); the listening answers are in
Tapescript §4 below. 1.8: build questions from a
grid (Can you / When do you × make/play/speak/
ride × English/cakes/a bike/tennis), then
**Write about yourself** as a gapped frame
modelled on Ali's Course Book autobiography
("__ am __ years old and live in __. I get up at
__ every day. I play / go __ on __ and play / go
__ on Wednesdays. I __ but I can't __.").

**Unit 2 (pp.12–19).** 2.1: ask a partner the
eight family questions, write short answers;
**Write full words** — expand I've got, I
haven't got, he's got, he's ten, I'm not
English, she hasn't got, they've got, it's hot,
we're Libyan (the Course Book's arrows run the
other way). 2.2: label **Omar's family tree**
from the key (grandmother = Maha, grandfather =
Mohammed, father = Ibrahim, mother = Nawal,
sister = Hind, brother = Salem); draw and talk
about your own tree. 2.3: job anagrams matched
to their tools (chertae, ordtoc, endsutt,
keeshporep, refamr, ernafim, urnes, olipcenam);
answer "What does Ali's father do? — He's a
doctor." and three more from the tool pictures.
2.4: listening ticks; write "It's Aisha's
pencil."-type possessive sentences from face +
object pairs. 2.5: match Whose/Is this/Are these
questions to their answers; **rewrite with one
word** — replace my juice / Ali's bike / his
flat / your pencils / the doctor's car / her
car with mine / his / his / yours / his / hers.
2.6: a reading text that exists only here —
**Mustafa's day**: "In the morning: Mustafa gets
up at quarter past six every day. He always
drinks juice for breakfast. He never drinks
coffee. His father sometimes drives him to
school in the car, but he sometimes walks. In
the evening: Mustafa plays football after school
on Tuesdays and Thursdays and he sometimes
watches television. He never goes swimming
because he can't swim." — then How-often
questions and writing your own day under the
same two headings. 2.7: vowel gaps (t_mp_r_t_r_,
h__d_ch_, st_m_ch_che) + build the illness
conversation from word fragments. 2.8: three
word-order questions; the listening names the
speaker (Tapescript §11: a teacher — "I live in
Benghazi and I work in a girl's school… I teach
English every morning").

**Unit 3 (pp.20–26).** 3.1: count dot piles to
match 13–20 and write the words (the -teen
numbers made countable); match the tens to 100.
3.2: a new price set in LD (books 80, watch 45,
camera 33, trousers 22, bike 80, bag 20) under
How much is/are questions. 3.3: **odd vowel out**
— "Which vowel sound is different in each
group?" with the vowel underlined (apple among
bun/hundred/number…); listen and write four
prices. 3.4: borrow conversations cloze (can/
can't/need/have/borrow/here), then write your
own for a glass of water, a pen, a rubber, a
ruler. 3.5: name eleven action pictures from a
verb bank; **-ing spelling by worked examples
in three groups** — plain (walk→walking…),
make→making (drop the e; write waits), swim→
swimming (double the letter; run waits) — then
Aisha listening ticks. 3.6: collocation gaps
from the 3.5 bank; a Look!: "I like swimming,
painting pictures **and** speaking English. I
don't like singing songs, making cakes **or**
playing tennis." — and/or named by use in
lists. 3.7: true/false listening on Salwa and
Aisha's wants; write a Fatima/Hala conversation
from cue words. 3.8: -ing vowel gaps + four
"I like / I don't like / I want to go / I
don't" sentences about yourself.

**Unit 4 (pp.27–34).** 4.1: furniture vowel
gaps (sh_lf, b_d, s_f_, w_nd_w, d__r, fl__r,
t_bl_, ch__r); write eight position sentences
from detail pictures with a word bank (behind,
in, in front of, next to, opposite, under, on —
"on" already crossed out by the worked example
"The radio is on the shelf."). 4.2: add one
more word to each room group (bathroom: toilet,
wash basin, +… — wash basin, cooker, lamp,
bookcase enter here); Where-is questions over
four room pictures. 4.3: listen-and-tick grids
for Aisha's new home, complete her gapped
description from your ticks, then write about
your home from three starters ("I live in a …
There's a … Our house has …") — the spoken
answers are Tapescript §19 below. 4.4:
transport vowel gaps with photographs (taxi,
bus, car, bike, **boat** — boat is new here);
word-order by-travel sentences; "How do you
travel to school?" 4.5: choose between/behind,
left/right, in front of/next to over a village
street scene; write six road signs' meanings.
4.6: **follow directions on the Course Book's
own town map**, reprinted here — listen and
draw your route, then read-and-draw ("Walk
along this street. Turn left at the supermarket
and walk past the café. Turn left and cross
over. Turn left again and walk straight on.
It's on your right."). 4.7: match three written
routes to three dotted routes on a second town
map. 4.8: write directions for a partner to
follow + category odd-one-out.

**Unit 5 (pp.35–41).** 5.1: clothes vowel gaps
(t__, tr_n_rs, b_lt, b__ts, sc_rf — **boots**
enter here) + colour the clothes and talk
("The tie is black and white."); write colour +
clothes phrases ("red trousers", a + word for
T-shirt/skirt/shirt). 5.2: listen and colour;
"What is Ali wearing? He is wearing…" +
Ahmed + your own clothes today. 5.3: the beach
picture again (Hasan, Ali, Nisrin, Mum, Dad
labelled) with -ing sentences to complete
("Dad is __ in the sea. Mum __ a magazine.
Nisrin __ orangeade. Hasan and Ali __
football."); a second pp.81/82 information gap
(Is Amna watching TV? — No, she isn't.). 5.4:
**-ing spelling tables grouped by pattern**
(eat→eating set · drive→driving set · swim→
swimming set), then circle four differences
between two café pictures and write them as
paired sentences ("In picture 1, the man is
reading a book. In picture 2, he is reading a
newspaper."). 5.5: a Look! on **two
adjectives** — "a long, blue dress / a short,
red dress" with discovery questions ("Where is
the colour adjective? What comes between the
adjectives?"); reorder three shopping questions
whose word tokens include the comma itself.
5.6: complete the **was/were table** by person
("Where __ I/you/he/she/we/you/they
yesterday?") and write Yesterday sentences
from building pictures. 5.7: grow four word
groups from a bank (T-shirt / small / shoe shop
/ wear); answer where you were at nine o'clock
yesterday morning, half past three yesterday
afternoon, ten o'clock yesterday evening,
Friday morning. 5.8: nine true/false statements
about the yellow-shirt story, cross-referenced
"the story on page 43–44 of your Course Book"
(the traps: seven dinars for six; too small
for too big).

**Unit 6 (pp.42–49).** 6.1: body-part anagrams
(rmas, gsle, eeft, ndsha, ckne, deah) matched to
pictures; listen to three animal descriptions
and tick among six **photographs** (fish,
giraffe, chimpanzee, tiger, cobra, kingfisher —
the kingfisher is new; answers in Tapescript
§28); describe two more animals with "It has
got … / It hasn't got … / It can … / It can't
…". 6.2: animal vowel gaps over photographs
(elephant, cheetah, butterfly, rabbit, camel,
goat, horse, snake, tiger, giraffe); complete
three animal descriptions from word boxes —
**Its** arrives in writing ("Its neck is long
and its head is small. Its legs are long and
it can run fast."). 6.3: which animals? from
habitat clues ("They can climb trees. —
monkeys · They always live in water. · We
sometimes get milk from these animals. · They
usually live in hot, dry countries. · You can
ride them. · They sometimes eat paper. · They
eat fruit and nuts. · They usually live in hot,
wet jungles."); opposites from a box (east–
west given; dry, hot, old, big, up, early,
finish, nasty wait); **find pairs of words
with the same sound** (walk – talk given; the
bank hides dry/fly, tree/we, goat/boat,
near/ear, wet/get, nut/but, bed/head). 6.4:
**correct the false sentences** about the
Course Book's desert picture ("There are four
camels. → There are three camels." … six
sentences; the workbook cites "Course Book
page 52" — see differences below); then answer
for yourself: "Where are you? What are you
doing? Who is sitting behind you?" 6.5:
listen and order three weather conversations,
tick the boxes (Mohammed is in Libya /
Manchester; It is snowing / raining…). 6.6:
odd one out by kind (season among spring/
autumn/winter; snow among sunny/windy/rainy…);
true/false about **the seasons in England**
("The winter is cold. T · Plants grow in the
winter. · The weather gets hot in the spring.
· Plants start to grow in the spring. · The
summer is hot and windy. · Fruit and
vegetables are ready to eat in the summer. ·
The weather is cold in the autumn. · The
leaves fall off the trees in the winter.") and
correct the false ones. 6.7: match the month
abbreviations to the full names; write the
months in order from January; six questions
incl. "When is the winter in Australia?" 6.8:
complete a **seasons in Libya** chart (Which
months? / What's the weather like? / What do
you see? × spring–winter; given: summer =
June–August, spring weather = windy), then
write about Libya's seasons from frames that
fade from gapped (spring, summer) to bare
headings (Autumn, Winter).

**Unit 7 (pp.50–56).** 7.1: listen to Fatima,
tick a telephone/radio/table-tennis × morning/
afternoon/evening grid, write the past
sentences (answers in Tapescript §34: morning
played table tennis with Aisha, afternoon
phoned Amna, evening listened to the radio).
7.2: correct six wrong sentences about Omar's
diary; reorder Were/Did questions; write a Did
question for a partner. 7.3: write the past of
go (went given), am/is, drink, buy, have,
drive, do; use them in seven picture-prompted
sentences (the yellow-shirt day retold); write
four sentences of your own. 7.4: build the
Aisha/Amna café conversation from a skeleton
with ticks and food pictures, then write it
out in full. 7.5: choose **very / too** in
five sentences ("I liked the chicken. It was
very / too good."); replace underlined
repeated nouns with He / It / She / We / They
(the writing twin of the Course Book's blue
**it** — one text has the grandparents in
**Benghazi**). 7.6: true/false on the park
story + correct the false + practise the
Ahmed/Mustafa phone conversation, printed
here only in its opening lines — its full
text is Tapescript §38 below. 7.7: grammar
gets its names — two Look! boxes: "**nouns**
are the names of things or people: Ahmed,
car · **pronouns** are words we use in place
of nouns: he, she · **verbs** tell you what
things or people do: go, didn't go" and
"**adjectives** describe people and things:
big, expensive, cold" — then sort twelve
words from the castle text (pot, we, drove,
they, castle, cooked, it, jewellery, Tripoli,
went, bought, she) into the three columns,
and choose the best adjective for five
sentences. 7.8: "Look at Course Book page 60.
Use the verbs in the box to write about Ali's
father. Use your exercise book." (got up, had,
drove, talked, read, went) — writing that
spills off the workbook onto loose paper; the
reference text is Tapescript §39.

**Unit 8 (pp.57–65).** 8.1: food spelling
with the quantity built into the caption
(some ch__se, a sw__t, some _gg_, some r_ce,
some br__d, two t_m_toe_); a/some café
dialogs to complete; **complete the words
with ch or sh** (__eetah, __oes, __op,
__icken, __ocolate, __ort, __eese, __irt,
__air, __elf). 8.2: match tea/water/sandwich/
coffee/biscuits to pictures; container phrases
(a cup of tea given; a packet of…, a glass
of…, a bottle of…); write **a**, **some** or a
number before nine food words. 8.3: listen
and tick a printed MENU for Hasan and Aisha,
then complete the some/any report (waiter's
"No, sorry. There aren't any grapes today."
is built into the frame; full order in
Tapescript §43). 8.4: split a word snake
(eggsoilflourbuttersugarbiscuits) and label
six pictures; complete five sentences about
the Course Book p.64 cake conversation; sort
23 food words into **Things you can count**
(one/two — "potato – potatoes" given) and
**Things you cannot count** (some — "cheese"
given); then fit the snake words after a
bottle of / a glass of / a bag of / a bunch
of / a kilo of / a litre of / a packet of…
and compare with a partner. 8.5: true/false
on the Italy phone call (CB p.65) + correct
the false + choose delicious/horrible/lucky/
good for four sentences. 8.6: **read three
people and decide who is healthy** — "1.
Naser doesn't like meat or fish. He hardly
ever eats fruit or vegetables. He eats sweets
and ice cream every day. He watches TV a lot.
2. Aisha likes fish, salad and fruit. She
sometimes drinks tea, but her favourite drink
is milk. She doesn't play any games, but she
often goes running three times a week. 3.
Khalid is always hungry. He eats lots of
bread, butter and cheese. His favourite meal
is chicken and rice. He doesn't eat many
vegetables. He never drinks water or milk. He
likes lemonade." — then **give advice** to the
unhealthy ones as imperatives ("Don't eat too
many sweets. · Eat more vegetables. · Go
swimming two or three times a week."). 8.7:
match chore verbs (make, tidy, sweep, clean,
water, wash, answer, look after) to their
objects, extend them (wash clothes, answer
questions, look after your sister, tidy your
desk…); a Look!: "tidy and clean can be
adjectives as well as verbs: Is your desk
tidy? Are your hands clean?"; a partner
chart — How often do you…? × always / often /
sometimes / hardly ever / never over five
chore pictures. 8.8: past forms of the chore
verbs (sweep → **swept** shown; make's past
is the irregular one to find), then answer
three questions about the Course Book
housework conversation — the workbook cites
"Course Book page 72" (see differences); the
conversation's full text is Tapescript §46.

**Unit 9 (pp.66–73).** 9.1: match 1st–12th to
first–twelfth; say 15th/20th/30th; write six
dates in words ("20th May → the twentieth of
May"). 9.2: **Mohammed's invitation** — a
letter that exists only here: "Dear Ahmed,
Would you like to come __ my party? The party
is __ my house. My birthday is __ Thursday,
but the party is __ Friday. It starts __ five
o'clock. It finishes __ seven o'clock. Best
wishes, Mohammed" — fill to/on/at; reorder
four party questions; write your own
invitation from a frame (__ is having a party
/ on __ / starts at __ / finishes at __ /
place __). 9.3: answer five questions on CB
p.71; make, then write, a suggesting
conversation on a printed skeleton ("What
shall we do today? — Let's go … — No, I did
that yesterday. — OK. Would you like to …? —
I don't think so. — OK. Shall we …? — That's
a good idea!"). 9.4: label present pictures
(a pair of sandals, a box of paints, a pair
of socks among them); assemble a choosing-a-
present conversation from four boxed
sentences ("How about a pair of socks? · How
old is he? · How about a box of paints? · How
about this belt?"). 9.5: listen and draw
lines — which present Aisha bought for older
sister / baby sister / mother / grandmother /
father / grandfather (answers in Tapescript
§50); then a **capital letters** Look!: "Use
Capital letters at the beginning of …
sentences, e.g., My new house is big. · names
of people, e.g., Mustafa, Amna · names of
places, e.g., Libya, England · names of days,
e.g., Friday — Don't forget! You need a full
stop at the end of a sentence." and rewrite
"ahmed is twelve" and "khalid goes fishing
every saturday" correctly. 9.6: **sounds and
letters: c, k, ck** — say could / kitchen /
truck, then complete _at, _ake, _amera,
_olour, _ar, _old, _ousin (c), _ilo (k),
ja__et, so__, bla__ (ck); answer "What do you
usually do at weekends? What did you do last
weekend?" 9.7: join the lake story's sentence
halves (CB p.75), checked by listening; a
Look! **Joining sentences – so and but**:
"Sam didn't come back. → Tom went to look for
him. / Sam didn't come back, so Tom went to
look for him. / Tom found Sam. → He couldn't
walk. / Tom found Sam, but he couldn't walk."
9.8: complete four sentences about Mohammed's
letter (is staying / will go / to meet / go).

**Unit 10 (pp.74–80).** 10.1: short answers
from the posters (CB p.77) incl. matching the
three opening times; choose a venue for three
people and say why; which would you like?
10.2: true/false on the CB p.78 conversation
+ correct the false + your sunny afternoons
and windy Fridays. 10.3: answer about the
long-neck dinosaur (CB p.79), then **write
the questions** for given answers ("It had
two arms. · It ate meat. · It was about
thirteen metres long."). 10.4: reorder five
museum questions; complete a museum
conversation by inventing the questions that
fit the printed answers (its last answer:
"Yes, I want to go to the Egyptian room next
time."); odd one out **with a spoken reason**
from a question bank (Can you read it? Can
you wear it? Do you drink it? Can you write
with it? Is it a colour? Do you eat it? Do
you cook it?) — "Ice-cream isn't fruit."
10.5: complete Ali's life in When-sentences
from its numbered pictures (CB p.81); listen
to Fatima and write the ages of her six life
events (answers in Tapescript §55). 10.6:
ask and answer about Fatima ("What happened
when Fatima was three? Her sister was
born."); **Now write about your life.** 10.7:
describe Ali's beach photograph in writing,
continuing his printed start, covering
Mustafa, Khalid, Omar, Hasan and the man;
definition riddles — "What are they? 1. You
use these to write with. … 4. An animal with
a very long neck. … 9. Animals from long ago.
10. Where you go when you are ill." 10.8:
ask and answer about Ali's favourite things
from pictures (sport → football; day →
**Friday**, circled on a calendar printed
Monday-first — as printed; meat, fruit,
drink, activity, animal likewise), then ask
your partner.

**Back matter (pp.81–103).** *Sound and

has different sounds" — sort bag, car, cat,
date, flat, plant, glass, grape, make, start,

**Differences from the Course Book, as
printed.** (1) The tapescript's park story
(§37) names its boy **Hasan** — "On Thursday,
Hasan left his house at quarter to five. He
walked to the park… At seven o'clock, Hasan
phoned Ahmed." — where the Course Book's
printed story, its §38 phone conversation,
and this workbook's own 7.6 questions all say
**Mustafa**. (2) Two of the workbook's Course
Book page references do not match the Course
Book's own numbering, whose other references
here are exact (pp.43–44, 60, 64, 65, 71, 75,
77, 78, 79, 81, 82): 6.4 cites "Course Book
page 52" for the desert picture, which is
lesson 6.4 at book p.48; 8.8 cites "Course
Book page 72" for the housework conversation,
which is lesson 8.8 at book p.68. (3) The
"Sound and spelling practice" section opens
at exercise C — no exercises A or B appear
in this book. (4) The Grade 7 pair otherwise
agree: Ali is eleven in both books' opening
material, twelve in his own story in both.

Record check first (the G4 lesson): the grade-08 record
(`~/workspace/phase3-staging/grade-08/gov/libya/books.json`) names
THREE English books — a **Course Book**
(`english/student-book.pdf`,
`https://liasinstitute.com/PDFs/08_English_for_Libya_Course_Book.pdf`),
a **Teacher Book** (`english/teacher-guide.pdf`,
`https://liasinstitute.com/PDFs/08_English_for_Libya_Teacher_Book.pdf`)
and a **Work Book** (`english/workbook.pdf`,
`https://liasinstitute.com/PDFs/08_English_for_Libya_Work_Book.pdf`),
all collected 2026-10-22, approval source Libya CERC. All three
downloaded 2026-10-25 into
`~/workspace/liqlearns-courses-work/libya-english/`: Course Book
**10,844,221 B, 122 pp** · Teacher Book **14,833,356 B, 154 pp** ·
Work Book **5,558,427 B, 98 pp** — all real %PDFs, all different
sizes and page counts from the G7 pair (88 / 104 pp), so no re-cover
on its face; the Course Book read below confirms a genuine new book.

**Series note:** Preparatory 2 wears the **"21st CENTURY" branding**
again — full-colour oasis cover photograph, "Garnet EDUCATION" —
and its imprint reads **"First edition, copyright © 2019 Garnet
Publishing Ltd"**: a genuinely new 2019 book in the same line as
Primary 1–5, NOT the 2007 course reprinted for Primary 6 and
Preparatory 1. The imprint page names no individual authors.

Audio; "For more resources, visit: **www.englishforlibya.com**";
